PART 309-315 — v1.36 / v8.27 PYTHON ADAPTATION: COMPLETE COMMON MISTAKES
================================================================================

PART 15 of Prompt_To_Ts.txt documents common mistakes in detail.
PART 213 in the Python spec has 11 mistakes; PART 309-315 here
expands to 30+ mistakes that cover the full Python failure surface.

The Python mistakes inherit the TS mistakes and add Python-
specific ones (Pyodide gotchas, MT bundle misuse, lbl.* edge
cases, etc.).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 309 — TYPOGRAPHY & SYNTAX MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 15.1)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  309.1 — WRONG FACTORY SIGNATURE
        ❌ def create_model():    # the renderer used to crash here
        ✅ def create_model(options=None):  # recommended
        # Both forms work since v1.36 patch 1, but options=None
        # is forward-compatible with future per-load options.

  309.2 — `import three` INSTEAD OF `from js import THREE`
        ❌ import three
        ❌ from three import *
        ✅ from js import THREE
        # Three.js is exposed via Pyodide's `js` module, not
        # a Python package.

  309.3 — FORGETTING `.new()` ON CONSTRUCTORS
        ❌ g = THREE.Group()        # returns the class!
        ✅ g = THREE.Group.new()    # Pyodide idiom
        # The parenthesised form returns the constructor itself.

  309.4 — USING TS-STYLE NEW()
        ❌ g = new THREE.Group()    # Python doesn't have `new`
        ✅ g = THREE.Group.new()
        # This is the inverse of 309.3.

  309.5 — MIXING CAMELCASE AND SNAKE_CASE
        ❌ lbl.attachBox("body", ...)   # camelCase TS form
        ✅ lbl.attach_box("body", ...)  # snake_case Python form
        # The lbl helper accepts both, but snake_case is the
        # Python convention. PART 246.3 documents the naming
        # convention.

  309.6 — FORGETTING `lbl` IMPORT
        ❌ attach_box("body", ...)     # NameError
        ✅ import lbl
           lbl.attach_box("body", ...)
        # Or use the auto-injected global: attach_box("body", ...)
        # The renderer auto-injects lbl into builtins, but
        # explicit import is more readable.

  309.7 — TYPO IN PALETTE NAME
        ❌ lbl.define_palette({"shel": {...}})  # typo
        ❌ lbl.attach_box("body", material="shell")  # missing key
        ✅ lbl.define_palette({"shell": {...}})
        # The lbl helper validates palette names; missing keys
        # throw a clear error at attach-time.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 310 — SEMANTIC & CONTRACT MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 15.2)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  310.1 — RETURNING A SINGLE MESH INSTEAD OF A GROUP
        ❌ return THREE.Mesh.new(geom, mat)
        ✅ g = THREE.Group.new(); g.add(mesh); return g
        # The renderer expects an Object3D with children.
        # A bare Mesh loses the ability to add more parts.

  310.2 — RETURNING A LIST INSTEAD OF A GROUP / DICT
        ❌ return [mesh1, mesh2, mesh3]   # type mismatch
        ✅ Style A: return lbl.blueprint()  # dict
        ✅ Style B: return g                 # Group
        # Lists and other types are rejected with a clear
        # error.

  310.3 — MISSING `castShadow` ON BODY-PART MESHES
        ❌ mesh.castShadow = False  # default in some Pyodide builds
        ✅ mesh.castShadow = True
           mesh.receiveShadow = True
        # Without shadows, the model looks "floating" against
        # a shadow-catcher floor.

  310.4 — FORGETTING TO REGISTER A TICK
        ❌ g = THREE.Group.new(); g.add(spinning_wheel)
        # Spinning wheel doesn't spin because no tick is registered.
        ✅ def tick(dt, elapsed):
               spinning_wheel.rotation.x += dt * 4.0
           g.userData.tick = tick
        # Style A (Blueprint-Return) doesn't support tick.

  310.5 — NOT POPULATING sculptRuntime
        ❌ g = THREE.Group.new()
           g.add(body, wheel)
           return g  # inspector's tree view is empty
        ✅ g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
               "nodes":  {"root": g, "body": body, "wheel": wheel},
               "meshes": {"body": body, "wheel": wheel},
           }
        # Without sculptRuntime, the inspector can't navigate
        # the model. See PART 207 for the full 9-key contract.

  310.6 — FLOATING-PARTS (amends PART 250)
        ❌ head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.2), mat)
           torso = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.2, 0.25, 0.5), mat)
           g.add(head, torso)  # visible gap at the neck!
        ✅ sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
               MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.7, 0], 0.2),
               MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.2, 0], [0, 0.6, 0], 0.2),
           )
           body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
           g.add(THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, mat))

  310.7 — STACKED CYLINDERS (amends PART 253)
        ❌ for i in range(3):
               seg = THREE.Mesh.new(
                   THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.08, 0.08, 0.08), mat_arm,
               )
               seg.position.y = -0.1 - i * 0.1
               arm.add(seg)
        ✅ profile = [
               {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.10},
               {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.07},
               {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05},
           ]
           arm_geo = MT_core.loft(profile, {"radial": 8, "length": 8})
           arm.add(THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, mat_arm))

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 311 — MATERIAL & TEXTURE MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 15.3)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  311.1 — SATURATED SMALL PART WITHOUT EMISSIVE
        ❌ "accent": {"color": "#f5821b"}  # small part, saturated
        # Validator warns (PART 50.3):
        #   ⚠ obj[N] "accent": saturated color on a small part
        #     with no emissive set.
        ✅ "glow": {
               "color": "#ffd66b",
               "emissive": "#ffae3a",
               "emissiveIntensity": 2.4,
           }
        # Or use a less-saturated color.

  311.2 — TRANSPARENT MATERIAL WITHOUT `transparent: True`
        ❌ mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
               "color": 0xffffff,
               "opacity": 0.5,  # ignored without transparent!
           })
        ✅ mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
               "color": 0xffffff,
               "transparent": True,
               "opacity": 0.5,
           })

  311.3 — METALLIC MATERIAL WITHOUT PMREM/HDRI
        ❌ mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
               "color": 0xcccccc,
               "metalness": 0.9,  # looks like flat plastic!
           })
        # The renderer's lookDevLights() factory auto-enables
        # PMREM/HDRI for metallic materials (PART 163). If
        # you're using a custom lookDevLights, you must enable
        # PMREM yourself:
        ✅ from js import THREE
           pmrem = THREE.PMREMGenerator.new(renderer)
           scene.environment = pmrem.fromScene(
               THREE.RoomEnvironment.new(), 0.04
           ).texture

  311.4 — WRONG COLOR FORMAT
        ❌ color: 0.5  # treated as black (0.0) by Three.js
        ✅ color: 0x808080  # hex int
        ✅ color: "#808080"  # hex string
        ✅ color: "gray"  # named CSS color
        # Python accepts all 3; pick the cleanest for the value.

  311.5 — CANVAS TEXTURE WITHOUT `needsUpdate`
        ❌ canvas = document.createElement("canvas")
           # ... draw on canvas ...
           tex = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(canvas)
           mat.map = tex
           # First frame may be blank.
        ✅ tex = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(canvas)
           tex.needsUpdate = True
           mat.map = tex
        # CanvasTexture needs needsUpdate=True after the
        # initial draw if the canvas was modified post-construct.

  311.6 — INCORRECT NORMAL MAP USAGE
        ❌ normal_canvas = ... # this is a HEIGHT map, not normal!
           mat.normalMap = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(normal_canvas)
           # The material gets wrong shading.
        ✅ height_canvas = ... # draw the height field
           normal_canvas = height_to_normal(height_canvas, strength=1.0)
           mat.normalMap = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(normal_canvas)
        # Use the height_to_normal helper (PART 298.1).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 312 — ANIMATION & RIGGING MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 15.4)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  312.1 — MISSING BONE PARENT
        ❌ bone = THREE.Bone.new()
           g.add(bone)  # orphan, not in skeleton
        ✅ skeleton = THREE.Skeleton.new([parent_bone, bone])
           for b in skeleton.bones:
               g.add(b)
        # A bone must be part of a Skeleton to deform a mesh.

  312.2 — NO SKINNED MESH BINDING
        ❌ skinned = THREE.SkinnedMesh.new(geom, mat)
           skinned.add(skeleton.bones[0])
           # No binding → mesh doesn't follow bones.
        ✅ skinned.bind(skeleton, skeleton.bones)
        # The SkinnedMesh needs .bind() to attach the skeleton.

  312.3 — ANIMATION CLIP WITHOUT MIXER
        ❌ g.animations = [clip]   # mixer never created
           g.userData.tick = lambda dt, elapsed: None
        ✅ mixer = THREE.AnimationMixer.new(g)
           action = mixer.clipAction(clip)
           action.play()
           g.userData.tick = lambda dt, elapsed: mixer.update(dt)
        # The renderer doesn't auto-create the mixer.

  312.4 — TICK WITH WRONG SIGNATURE
        ❌ def tick():  # no args
               g.rotation.y += 0.01
        ✅ def tick(dt, elapsed):
               g.rotation.y += dt * 0.5
        # The renderer calls tick with (dt, elapsed). No-arg
        # ticks raise TypeError.

  312.5 — STATE IN MODULE-LEVEL VARIABLES
        ❌ counter = 0
           def create_model():
               global counter
               counter += 1
        ✅ def create_model():
               return _build_with_state({"run_id": 1})
        # The renderer doesn't reset module globals. Use
        # closures or pass state through the return value.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 313 — STAGE / SCALE / POSE MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 213 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 15.5)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  313.1 — MODEL OUTSIDE [-1, +1] BOUNDS
        ❌ # All primitives 10x too large
           box = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(10, 10, 10)
           # Renderer auto-frames but the model looks tiny in the viewport.
        ✅ # Pick a convention: bbox fits in [-1, +1]
           box = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(1, 1, 1)

  313.2 — MODEL CENTERED ON (0, 0, 0) INSTEAD OF BOTTOM
        ❌ # All meshes centered on origin
           # Result: model floats above the ground plane
        ✅ # Anchor the bottom of the bbox to Y=0
           body.position.y = body_height / 2

  313.3 — INCONSISTENT UNITS
        ❌ body = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(100, 50, 30)  # cm
           wheel = THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.3, 0.3, 0.2)  # m
        ✅ # Pick a unit (metres is canonical) and stick to it
           body = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(1.0, 0.5, 0.3)
           wheel = THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.3, 0.3, 0.2)

  313.4 — MISSING LIGHTING
        ❌ g = THREE.Group.new()
           g.add(mesh)
           return g  # invisible without lights!
        ✅ # The renderer's lookDevLights() factory auto-applies
           # lights based on meta.style. Override only if you
           # need a specific look:
           g.userData.customLookDevLights = my_lights

  313.5 — MODEL NOT GROUND-CONTACT
        ❌ # Wheel center at y=0.25, radius 0.25 → ground at y=0
           # but body bottom at y=0.3 → model looks "floating"
        ✅ # Either lower the body or raise the wheel center
           wheel.position.y = 0.25  # bottom of wheel at y=0
           body.position.y = 0.5 + 0.05  # body bottom at y=0.5

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 314 — VALIDATION & DEBUG MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  314.1 — IGNORING VALIDATOR WARNINGS
        ❌ "OK these warnings are non-blocking, ship it"
        # Many warnings are silent failures: the model
        # technically works but has visual regressions.
        ✅ # Run lblValidateAgainstSpec() and address every
           # BLOCK warning. Address MEDIUM warnings when
           # possible. LOW warnings are best-effort.

  314.2 — NOT USING THE IoU CHECK
        ❌ return g  # No IoU check, no retry
        # Image-driven factories that skip the IoU check
        # produce models that drift from the reference.
        ✅ iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
           if iou < 0.80:
               g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
        # See PART 254 for the full Python form.

  314.3 — PRINT() LEFT IN PRODUCTION CODE
        ❌ print("loading model...")
           print(f"scale = {scale}")
        # Pyodide captures stdout into the validator log.
        # A noisy print() clutters the log.
        ✅ # Save print() for actual runtime diagnostics:
           if iou < 0.50:
               print(f"Warning: IoU critically low ({iou})")

  314.4 — USING sys.exit() IN A FACTORY
        ❌ if some_error:
               sys.exit(1)  # kills the whole renderer!
        ✅ if some_error:
               raise ValueError("descriptive message")
        # Pyodide runs in a sandbox; sys.exit() can crash the
        # page. Use exceptions instead.

  314.5 — BLOCKING CALLS (time.sleep, etc.)
        ❌ import time
           time.sleep(2)  # freezes the renderer for 2s
        ✅ # No blocking calls in factory. If you need
           # async (e.g. importExternalMesh), use asyncio:
           import asyncio
           g = await asyncio.ensure_future(
               lbl.import_external_mesh("...")
           )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 315 — STYLE & METADATA MISTAKES (PYTHON FORM)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  315.1 — WRONG META.STYLE
        ❌ bp["meta"]["style"] = "LowPoly"  # capitalized
        # Validator warns (PART 48.1): not in canonical list
        ✅ bp["meta"]["style"] = "low-poly"  # canonical
        # 13 canonical styles: "smooth-low-poly" (default),
        # "low-poly", "flat-low-poly", "voxel", "chibi",
        # "anime-cel", "pbr-realistic", "papercraft", "toon",
        # "photogrammetry", "isometric-flat", "low-poly-painterly",
        # "wireframe", "x-ray".

  315.2 — WRONG META.CATEGORY
        ❌ bp["meta"]["category"] = "thing"  # not canonical
        ✅ bp["meta"]["category"] = "prop"  # canonical catch-all
        # 11 canonical categories: "character", "vehicle",
        # "architecture", "weapon", "creature", "plant",
        # "furniture", "prop", "environment", "abstract", "other".

  315.3 — MISSING PROPORTION DECLARATION
        ❌ return lbl.blueprint()  # auto-injects defaults, but no comments
        ✅ # STYLE: smooth-low-poly
           # CAMERA: isometric-front-right
           # SUBJECT_CATEGORY: character
           # ... 10 lines total ...
           return lbl.blueprint()
        # The text-level declaration is required for PART 49.1.

  315.4 — VERSION STAMP WRONG
        ❌ bp["meta"]["lblVersion"] = "v1.0"  # way out of date
        ✅ bp["meta"]["lblVersion"] = "v1.36 / v8.27"
        # The renderer checks meta.lblVersion; if it doesn't
        # match the current canonical pair, it warns.

  315.5 — DETAIL INVENTORY OUT OF SYNC
        ❌ g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
               "detailInventory": [
                   {"id": "wing_L", "region": "body",
                    "kind": "feature", "priority": "high", ...},
               ],
           }
           # But no mesh with `name = "wing_L"` in g.children!
        ✅ # Every high-priority detailInventory entry must
           # match at least one Mesh in the userData tree.
           g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
               "detailInventory": [...],
               "meshes": {"wing_L": wing_L_mesh, ...},
               "nodes":  {"wing_L": wing_L_pivot, ...},
           }
        # The validator's PART 207 audit catches this.

================================================================================
PART 316-322 — v1.36 / v8.27 PYTHON WORKED EXAMPLES (5 NEW)
================================================================================

The original v1.36 / v8.27 release shipped 2 Python worked examples
(PART 211 hello_block, PART 212 tiny_motorcycle). The TS spec
ships 5+ worked examples. PART 316-322 here adds 5 more
comprehensive Python examples spanning the 5 archetypes.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 316 — CHUNKY ROBOT (CHUNKY-MASCOT)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_robot.py — Chunky 3D mascot robot.
  Style: chunky-mascot, scene: a friendly 5-piece robot.
  Demonstrates: PART 250 (SDF) + PART 251 (bbox pivots) +
  PART 253 (loft) + PART 252 (flatShading) + PART 254 (IoU if
  image-driven).
  """
  from js import MT_core, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Robot"

      # Palette
      shell_mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x5a7fb8, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.1,
          "flatShading": True,  # PART 252
      })
      accent_mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0xf5821b, "roughness": 0.5, "metalness": 0.1,
          "flatShading": True,
      })
      eye_mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0xffffff, "roughness": 0.2,
          "emissive": 0xaef5ff, "emissiveIntensity": 1.5,
          "flatShading": True,
      })

      # Body — SDF-merged (PART 250)
      sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.4, 0], 0.3),
          MT_core.sdfCapsule([0, 0.0, 0], [0, 0.3, 0], 0.2),
      )
      body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 64})
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, shell_mat)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Head — separate primitive
      head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.4, 0.35, 0.4), shell_mat)
      head.position.y = 0.75
      head.castShadow = True
      head.name = "head"
      g.add(head)

      # Eyes
      for i, x in enumerate((-0.08, 0.08)):
          eye = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.05, 8, 6), eye_mat)
          eye.position.set(x, 0.78, 0.21)
          eye.name = f"eye_{i}"
          g.add(eye)

      # Arms — lofted (PART 253)
      arm_profile = [
          {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.07},  # shoulder
          {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.05},  # elbow
          {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.04},  # wrist
      ]
      arm_geo = MT_core.loft(arm_profile, {"radial": 8, "length": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          arm = THREE.Mesh.new(arm_geo, shell_mat)
          # Bbox-derived pivot (PART 251)
          body.geometry.computeBoundingBox()
          torso_w = body.geometry.boundingBox.max.x - body.geometry.boundingBox.min.x
          arm.position.set(side * torso_w * 0.55, 0.35, 0)
          arm.castShadow = True
          arm.name = f"arm_{side}"
          g.add(arm)

      # Legs
      leg_profile = [
          {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.08},
          {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.06},
          {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05},
      ]
      leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 8, "length": 8})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, shell_mat)
          leg.position.set(side * 0.12, -0.4, 0)
          leg.castShadow = True
          leg.name = f"leg_{side}"
          g.add(leg)

      # Antenna
      antenna = THREE.Mesh.new(
          THREE.CylinderGeometry.new(0.01, 0.01, 0.15, 6), accent_mat,
      )
      antenna.position.set(0, 0.97, 0)
      antenna.name = "antenna"
      g.add(antenna)
      antenna_tip = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.04, 8, 6), accent_mat)
      antenna_tip.position.set(0, 1.07, 0)
      antenna_tip.name = "antenna_tip"
      g.add(antenna_tip)

      # sculptRuntime (PART 207)
      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "eye_0", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "eye_1", "region": "face", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "arm_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "arm_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "leg_-1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "leg_1", "region": "limb", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "antenna", "region": "head", "kind": "accessory",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.4},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "character",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.8, "fill": 0.78,
                          "head_share_of_length": 0.3},
      }
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 317 — ORGANIC CREATURE (CHITIN)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  beetle_creature.py — Chunky beetle with iridescent shell.
  Style: chunky-mascot, archetype: creature.
  Demonstrates: PART 286 chitin material + MT_organic helpers
  (none — pure static mesh) + PART 31 sculpt enrichment.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_materials, MT_advanced, THREE

  def create_model():
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Iridescent Beetle"

      # Materials — chitin recipe from PART 286
      chitin = THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x261a0f, "roughness": 0.35, "metalness": 0.2,
          "iridescence": 1.0, "iridescenceIOR": 1.3,
          "iridescenceThicknessRange": [200, 800],
          "clearcoat": 0.6, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1,
      })
      wing = THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x0a2540, "roughness": 0.2, "metalness": 0.3,
          "iridescence": 1.0, "iridescenceIOR": 1.5,
          "iridescenceThicknessRange": [300, 1000],
          "transparent": True, "opacity": 0.85,
          "side": THREE.DoubleSide,
      })

      # Body — single SDF-merged shell
      sdf = MT_core.sdfUnion(
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.15, 0], 0.18),
          MT_core.sdfSphere([0, 0.0, 0.05], 0.15),
      )
      body_geo = MT_core.sdfMarch(sdf, {"resolution": 96})
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, chitin)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Head
      head = THREE.Mesh.new(THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.10, 16, 12), chitin)
      head.position.set(0, 0.18, 0.16)
      head.castShadow = True
      head.name = "head"
      g.add(head)

      # Elytra (wing covers) — 2 thin shells on the back
      for side in (-1, 1):
          elytron_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("sphere", {
              "r": 0.16, "ws": 16, "hs": 8, "phiStart": 0,
              "phiLength": math.pi / 2, "thetaStart": 0,
              "thetaLength": math.pi,
          })
          elytron = THREE.Mesh.new(elytron_geo, chitin)
          elytron.scale.set(0.9, 0.5, 0.4)
          elytron.position.set(side * 0.05, 0.18, -0.05)
          elytron.rotation.y = side * math.pi / 4
          elytron.castShadow = True
          elytron.name = f"elytron_{side}"
          g.add(elytron)

      # Legs — 6 lofted legs
      leg_profile = [
          {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.025},
          {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.018},
          {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.012},
      ]
      leg_geo = MT_core.loft(leg_profile, {"radial": 6, "length": 6})
      for side in (-1, 1):
          for i in range(3):
              leg = THREE.Mesh.new(leg_geo, chitin)
              x = side * (0.10 + i * 0.05)
              z = 0.10 - i * 0.05
              leg.position.set(x, 0.02, z)
              leg.rotation.z = side * (0.3 + i * 0.2)
              leg.castShadow = True
              leg.name = f"leg_{side}_{i}"
              g.add(leg)

      # Instanced eyes (2 tiny spheres)
      eye_geo = THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.025, 8, 6)
      eyes = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(eye_geo, 2, {
          "transforms": [
              {"position": [-0.03, 0.20, 0.25], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
               "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
              {"position": [0.03, 0.20, 0.25], "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
               "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
          ],
      })
      g.add(eyes)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {"body": body, "head": head, "elytra": eyes, "legs": g.children[3:9]},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "thorax", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "elytra", "region": "wings", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "legs", "region": "limbs", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "creature",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.2, "fill": 0.6},
      }
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 318 — CHUNKY CHARACTER WITH RIG (WALK CYCLE)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  walk_cycle_character.py — A rigged chunky character with a
  walk cycle. Demonstrates: SkinnedMesh + AnimationMixer +
  per-frame tick (Style B).
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_organic, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Walking Character"

      # Materials
      skin = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0xf2a07b, "roughness": 0.6, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      cloth = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x4a6fa5, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Build a base body mesh
      body_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("capsule", {"r": 0.15, "length": 0.5,
                                                    "capSegments": 8,
                                                    "radialSegments": 16})
      head_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("sphere", {"r": 0.12, "ws": 16, "hs": 12})

      # SkinnedMesh + skeleton
      skeleton = THREE.Skeleton.new([
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 0: pelvis
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 1: spine
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 2: chest
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 3: head
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 4: arm_L
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 5: arm_R
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 6: leg_L
          THREE.Bone.new(),  # 7: leg_R
      ])
      # Position the bones
      bones = skeleton.bones
      bones[0].position.set(0, 0, 0)        # pelvis
      bones[1].position.set(0, 0.25, 0)     # spine
      bones[2].position.set(0, 0.45, 0)     # chest
      bones[3].position.set(0, 0.6, 0)      # head
      bones[4].position.set(0.15, 0.45, 0)  # arm_L shoulder
      bones[5].position.set(-0.15, 0.45, 0) # arm_R shoulder
      bones[6].position.set(0.07, 0.0, 0)   # leg_L hip
      bones[7].position.set(-0.07, 0.0, 0)  # leg_R hip

      # Build the skinned body
      body_mesh = THREE.SkinnedMesh.new(body_geo, cloth)
      for b in bones:
          body_mesh.add(b)
      body_mesh.bind(skeleton, bones)
      body_mesh.name = "body"
      g.add(body_mesh)

      # Animation: walk cycle
      mixer = THREE.AnimationMixer.new(g)
      times = [0, 0.5, 1.0]
      # Arm_L
      track_arm_L = THREE.NumberKeyframeTrack.new(
          "bones[4].rotation[x]", times,
          [0, 0.4, 0],
      )
      # Arm_R (opposite phase)
      track_arm_R = THREE.NumberKeyframeTrack.new(
          "bones[5].rotation[x]", times,
          [0, -0.4, 0],
      )
      # Leg_L
      track_leg_L = THREE.NumberKeyframeTrack.new(
          "bones[6].rotation[x]", times,
          [0, -0.3, 0],
      )
      # Leg_R
      track_leg_R = THREE.NumberKeyframeTrack.new(
          "bones[7].rotation[x]", times,
          [0, 0.3, 0],
      )
      clip = THREE.AnimationClip.new("walk", 1.0, [
          track_arm_L, track_arm_R, track_leg_L, track_leg_R,
      ])
      action = mixer.clipAction(clip)
      action.play()
      g.userData.tick = lambda dt, elapsed: mixer.update(dt)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {b.name or f"bone_{i}": b for i, b in enumerate(bones)},
          "meshes": {"body": body_mesh},
          "animation": {"walk": action},
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "character",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.75,
                          "head_share_of_length": 0.25},
      }
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 319 — CHUNKY VEHICLE (FORM-PRESERVED)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_sedan.py — A 4-door sedan, form-preserved (PART 179
  Hierarchy of Fidelity). Demonstrates: lathed wheels (no flat
  cylinders!) + curved fairing via ExtrudeGeometry + bezier.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_hardsurface, MT_materials, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Sedan"

      # Palette
      paint = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0xcc4444, "roughness": 0.4, "metalness": 0.3,
          "clearcoat": 0.7, "clearcoatRoughness": 0.1,
      })
      tire = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x1a1a1e, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      glass = THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x90c0e0, "transmission": 0.85, "thickness": 0.05,
          "roughness": 0.05, "transparent": True, "opacity": 0.5,
      })

      # Body — extruded 2D shape with rounded top
      body_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      body_shape.moveTo(-1.0, 0)
      body_shape.lineTo(1.0, 0)
      body_shape.bezierCurveTo(1.05, 0.2, 1.0, 0.45, 0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape.lineTo(-0.7, 0.5)
      body_shape.bezierCurveTo(-1.0, 0.5, -1.05, 0.2, -1.0, 0)
      body_shape.closePath()
      body_geo = THREE.ExtrudeGeometry.new(body_shape, {
          "depth": 0.8, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.04, "bevelThickness": 0.04, "bevelSegments": 4,
      })
      body = THREE.Mesh.new(body_geo, paint)
      body.position.set(0, 0.3, 0)
      body.castShadow = True
      body.receiveShadow = True
      body.name = "body"
      g.add(body)

      # Cabin — smaller extruded shape on top
      cabin_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      cabin_shape.moveTo(-0.55, 0)
      cabin_shape.lineTo(0.55, 0)
      cabin_shape.bezierCurveTo(0.65, 0.1, 0.65, 0.25, 0.5, 0.3)
      cabin_shape.lineTo(-0.5, 0.3)
      cabin_shape.bezierCurveTo(-0.65, 0.25, -0.65, 0.1, -0.55, 0)
      cabin_shape.closePath()
      cabin_geo = THREE.ExtrudeGeometry.new(cabin_shape, {
          "depth": 0.7, "bevelEnabled": True,
          "bevelSize": 0.03, "bevelThickness": 0.03, "bevelSegments": 3,
      })
      cabin = THREE.Mesh.new(cabin_geo, paint)
      cabin.position.set(-0.05, 0.55, 0)
      cabin.castShadow = True
      cabin.name = "cabin"
      g.add(cabin)

      # Windows — extruded shape with cabin-shape hole
      window_shape = THREE.Shape.new()
      window_shape.moveTo(-0.5, 0)
      window_shape.lineTo(0.5, 0)
      window_shape.bezierCurveTo(0.55, 0.05, 0.55, 0.2, 0.45, 0.25)
      window_shape.lineTo(-0.45, 0.25)
      window_shape.bezierCurveTo(-0.55, 0.2, -0.55, 0.05, -0.5, 0)
      window_shape.closePath()
      # Hole (cutout)
      hole = THREE.Path.new()
      hole.moveTo(-0.45, 0.02)
      hole.lineTo(0.45, 0.02)
      hole.bezierCurveTo(0.48, 0.06, 0.48, 0.18, 0.42, 0.22)
      hole.lineTo(-0.42, 0.22)
      hole.bezierCurveTo(-0.48, 0.18, -0.48, 0.06, -0.45, 0.02)
      window_shape.holes.push(hole)
      window_geo = THREE.ExtrudeGeometry.new(window_shape, {
          "depth": 0.72, "bevelEnabled": False,
      })
      windows = THREE.Mesh.new(window_geo, glass)
      windows.position.set(-0.05, 0.55, 0)
      windows.name = "windows"
      g.add(windows)

      # Wheels — LATHED, not flat cylinders (PART 179.2 / E41)
      for i, x in enumerate((-0.7, 0.7)):
          wheel_geo = MT_hardsurface.createLathedTireGeometry({
              "outerR": 0.28, "innerR": 0.16, "width": 0.18,
              "treadDepth": 0.012, "sidewallStyle": "chunky",
          })
          wheel = THREE.Mesh.new(wheel_geo, tire)
          wheel.position.set(x, 0.28, 0)
          wheel.rotation.x = math.pi / 2  # axis along Z
          wheel.castShadow = True
          wheel.name = f"wheel_{i}"
          g.add(wheel)

      # Headlights
      for i, x in enumerate((-0.9, 0.9)):
          headlight = THREE.Mesh.new(
              THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.08, 12, 8),
              THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
                  "color": 0xffeecc,
                  "emissive": 0xffd66b, "emissiveIntensity": 2.0,
              }),
          )
          headlight.position.set(x, 0.4, 0.41)
          headlight.name = f"headlight_{i}"
          g.add(headlight)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in g.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in g.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "body", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "cabin", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
              {"id": "windows", "region": "chassis", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "wheel_0", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "wheel_1", "region": "running_gear", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.8},
              {"id": "headlight_0", "region": "front", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "headlight_1", "region": "front", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "vehicle",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.7,
                          "wheel_radius_over_H": 0.15},
      }
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 320 — BUILDING (4-WALL HOUSE)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_house.py — A 4-wall chunky house with door, windows,
  gable roof. Demonstrates: MT_architectural helpers + STACK_INDEX
  anti-Z-fighting + 4-mode look-dev lighting.
  """
  from js import MT_architectural, MT_lookdev, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      house = THREE.Group.new()
      house.name = "Chunky House"

      # Materials from the architectural palette
      palette = MT_architectural.materialPalette()
      wall_face = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new(palette["wall_face"])
      roof_tile = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new(palette["roof_tile"])
      door = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new(palette["door"])
      window_glass = THREE.MeshPhysicalMaterial.new(palette["window_glass"])

      # 4 walls
      for i, side in enumerate(("N", "E", "S", "W")):
          apertures = (
              [{"type": "door", "x": 0.5, "width": 0.9, "height": 2.1}]
              if side == "S" else
              [{"type": "window", "x": 1.5, "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8},
               {"type": "window", "x": 2.7, "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8}]
              if side == "N" else []
          )
          wall_geo = MT_architectural.createWallWithApertures({
              "width": 4.0, "height": 2.8, "thickness": 0.15,
              "apertures": apertures,
          })
          wall = THREE.Mesh.new(wall_geo, wall_face)
          wall.rotation.y = i * (math.pi / 2)
          wall.castShadow = True
          wall.receiveShadow = True
          wall.name = f"wall_{side}"
          house.add(wall)

      # Gable roof
      roof_geo = MT_architectural.createPitchedGableRoof({
          "width": 4.3, "length": 4.3, "pitch": 30, "tileRows": 8,
      })
      roof = THREE.Mesh.new(roof_geo, roof_tile)
      roof.position.y = 2.8
      roof.castShadow = True
      roof.receiveShadow = True
      roof.name = "roof"
      house.add(roof)

      # Door
      door_geo = MT_architectural.createHingedDoorUnit({
          "width": 0.9, "height": 2.1, "thickness": 0.05,
          "hinge": "right",
      })
      door_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(door_geo, door)
      door_mesh.position.set(0, 0, 2.0)  # south wall
      door_mesh.name = "door"
      house.add(door_mesh)

      # Windows
      for i, x in enumerate((1.5, 2.7)):
          win_geo = MT_architectural.createGlazedWindowUnit({
              "width": 0.8, "height": 0.8, "frameDepth": 0.05,
          })
          win = THREE.Mesh.new(win_geo, window_glass)
          win.position.set(x, 1.2, -2.0)  # north wall
          win.name = f"window_{i}"
          house.add(win)

      # sculptRuntime
      house.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {n.name: n for n in house.children if n.name},
          "meshes": {n.name: n for n in house.children
                     if n.name and hasattr(n, "geometry")},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "wall_N", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_S", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_E", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "wall_W", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "roof", "region": "roof", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "door", "region": "entrance", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.4},
              {"id": "window_0", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.3},
              {"id": "window_1", "region": "facade", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.3},
          ],
      }
      house.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "architecture",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.4, "fill": 0.85},
      }
      return house

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 321 — ENVIRONMENT (TREE + GROUND)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_tree.py — A low-poly tree with procedural foliage.
  Style: chunky-mascot, archetype: environment.
  Demonstrates: MT_organic.hairStrands alternative (procedural
  leaves) + instanced foliage.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_advanced, MT_materials, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      g = THREE.Group.new()
      g.name = "Chunky Tree"

      # Materials
      bark = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x5a3a1a, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      leaf = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x3a8a3a, "roughness": 0.7, "metalness": 0.0,
      })

      # Trunk — lofted
      trunk_profile = [
          {"t": 0.0, "radius": 0.10},
          {"t": 0.5, "radius": 0.07},
          {"t": 1.0, "radius": 0.05},
      ]
      trunk_geo = MT_core.loft(trunk_profile, {"radial": 8, "length": 8})
      trunk = THREE.Mesh.new(trunk_geo, bark)
      trunk.position.y = 0.5
      trunk.castShadow = True
      trunk.name = "trunk"
      g.add(trunk)

      # Foliage — instanced icosahedra
      leaf_geo = MT_core.makePrimitive("icosahedron", {"r": 0.25, "detail": 0})
      foliage = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(leaf_geo, 5, {
          "transforms": [
              {"position": [0, 1.2, 0], "rotation": [0, 0, 0], "scale": [1, 1, 1]},
              {"position": [0.2, 1.4, 0.1], "rotation": [0.5, 0, 0], "scale": [0.8, 0.8, 0.8]},
              {"position": [-0.15, 1.35, -0.1], "rotation": [0, 0.5, 0], "scale": [0.7, 0.7, 0.7]},
              {"position": [0.1, 1.6, -0.15], "rotation": [0.3, 0.3, 0], "scale": [0.6, 0.6, 0.6]},
              {"position": [-0.05, 1.5, 0.15], "rotation": [0, 0, 0.3], "scale": [0.65, 0.65, 0.65]},
          ],
      })
      g.add(foliage)

      g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {"trunk": trunk, "foliage": foliage},
          "meshes": {"trunk": trunk, "foliage": foliage},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "trunk", "region": "trunk", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
              {"id": "foliage", "region": "canopy", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
          ],
      }
      g.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "plant",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 0.5, "fill": 0.4,
                          "trunk_share_of_height": 0.4},
      }
      return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 322 — DENSE CITYSCAPE (INSTANCED BUILDINGS)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  """
  chunky_city.py — A dense cityscape of instanced buildings.
  Style: chunky-mascot, archetype: environment.
  Demonstrates: heavy instancing for performance + variation.
  """
  from js import MT_core, MT_advanced, MT_architectural, THREE
  import math

  def create_model():
      city = THREE.Group.new()
      city.name = "Chunky City"

      # Materials
      concrete = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0xa0a0a0, "roughness": 0.95, "metalness": 0.0,
      })
      glass = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
          "color": 0x6090b0, "roughness": 0.1, "metalness": 0.6,
          "transparent": True, "opacity": 0.6,
      })

      # 3 building archetypes
      bldg_geo_small = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.5, 0.8, 0.5)
      bldg_geo_med = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.7, 1.5, 0.7)
      bldg_geo_large = THREE.BoxGeometry.new(0.6, 2.5, 0.6)

      # Place buildings in a grid with variation
      buildings = []
      for x in range(-5, 6, 1):
          for z in range(-5, 6, 1):
              if abs(x) + abs(z) < 2:
                  continue  # leave a plaza in the center
              # Random size
              r = (x * 7 + z * 13) % 100 / 100.0
              geo = bldg_geo_small if r < 0.4 else (
                  bldg_geo_med if r < 0.8 else bldg_geo_large,
              )
              h = geo.parameters.height
              buildings.append({
                  "position": [x * 0.9, h / 2, z * 0.9],
                  "rotation": [0, 0, 0],
                  "scale": [1, 1, 1],
              })

      # Create 3 InstancedMesh (one per size)
      small_mesh = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(bldg_geo_small, 0, {})
      # ... actually fill in the count
      small_count = sum(1 for b in buildings
                        if b["position"][1] * 2 == 0.8)
      small_mesh = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(bldg_geo_small, small_count, {
          "transforms": [b for b in buildings if b["position"][1] * 2 == 0.8],
      })
      small_mesh.material = concrete
      small_mesh.castShadow = True
      city.add(small_mesh)

      med_count = sum(1 for b in buildings
                      if b["position"][1] * 2 == 1.5)
      med_mesh = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(bldg_geo_med, med_count, {
          "transforms": [b for b in buildings if b["position"][1] * 2 == 1.5],
      })
      med_mesh.material = concrete
      med_mesh.castShadow = True
      city.add(med_mesh)

      large_count = sum(1 for b in buildings
                        if b["position"][1] * 2 == 2.5)
      large_mesh = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(bldg_geo_large, large_count, {
          "transforms": [b for b in buildings if b["position"][1] * 2 == 2.5],
      })
      large_mesh.material = concrete
      large_mesh.castShadow = True
      city.add(large_mesh)

      city.userData.sculptRuntime = {
          "nodes": {"small": small_mesh, "med": med_mesh, "large": large_mesh},
          "meshes": {"small": small_mesh, "med": med_mesh, "large": large_mesh},
          "detailInventory": [
              {"id": "small", "region": "city", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.4},
              {"id": "med", "region": "city", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "medium", "reviewThreshold": 0.5},
              {"id": "large", "region": "city", "kind": "feature",
               "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.6},
          ],
      }
      city.userData.meta = {
          "style": "chunky-mascot",
          "category": "environment",
          "proportions": {"W_over_H": 1.0, "fill": 0.4},
      }
      return city

================================================================================
PART 323-330 — v1.36 / v8.27 PYTHON ADAPTATION: PRODUCTION DISCIPLINE
================================================================================

PART 38-45 of Prompt_To_Ts.txt cover the production discipline
character pipeline. PART 323-330 here is the Python adaptation:
the 60/25/15 polygon distribution, the 5-stage pipeline, the
11-step recipe, the variant ranking, the 7 production techniques,
and the GLB-first professional pipeline.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 323 — 60/25/15 POLYGON DISTRIBUTION (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 293.6 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 42.5)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 60/25/15 distribution is the canonical triangle allocation
  for a chunky-mascot model. Python form:

       from js import MT_core

       def allocate_tri_budget(total_tris, archetype="chunky_mascot"):
           """Allocate triangle budget across IDENTITY / SECONDARY / TERTIARY."""
           if archetype == "chunky_mascot":
               return {
                   "IDENTITY":   int(total_tris * 0.60),  # 60% — main subject
                   "SECONDARY":  int(total_tris * 0.25),  # 25% — supporting
                   "TERTIARY":   int(total_tris * 0.15),  # 15% — small details
               }
           elif archetype == "vehicle":
               return {
                   "IDENTITY":   int(total_tris * 0.50),
                   "SECONDARY":  int(total_tris * 0.30),
                   "TERTIARY":   int(total_tris * 0.20),
               }
           else:
               return {
                   "IDENTITY":   int(total_tris * 0.55),
                   "SECONDARY":  int(total_tris * 0.30),
                   "TERTIARY":   int(total_tris * 0.15),
               }

  Example: 5000 tris for a chunky mascot:
       budget = allocate_tri_budget(5000, "chunky_mascot")
       # IDENTITY: 3000, SECONDARY: 1250, TERTIARY: 750

  Detail inventory mapping:
       detail_inventory = [
           # IDENTITY (60%, 3000 tris total)
           {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
            "priority": "high", "triShare": 0.30, "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
           {"id": "body", "region": "torso", "kind": "feature",

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 324 — 5-STAGE PIPELINE (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 96-100)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5-stage pipeline from PART 96-100, in Python form:

       STAGES = ["LOW", "MID", "HIGH", "FULL", "DONE"]

       def build_5_stage_pipeline(stage, factory_func, *args):
           """Apply the 5-stage pipeline to a factory."""
           g = factory_func(*args)
           if stage == "LOW":
               # Just the silhouette
               for m in g.children:
                   if hasattr(m, "geometry"):
                       m.material = LOW_POLY_MAT
           elif stage == "MID":
               # Add material variety
               pass  # factory handles this
           elif stage == "HIGH":
               # Add detail meshes (rivets, decals)
               rivets = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(rivet_geo, 8, {...})
               g.add(rivets)
           elif stage == "FULL":
               # Add cast shadows + receive shadows
               for m in g.children:
                   m.castShadow = True
                   m.receiveShadow = True
           elif stage == "DONE":
               # Final audit
               audit = lblSpec.auditProductionCompleteness(g)
               return g
           return g

  Most Python factories use the 5-stage pipeline implicitly via
  the renderer's auto-application: the renderer reads meta.style
  and applies the matching stage. Use the explicit form only when
  you need fine control.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 325 — 11-STEP RECIPE (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 165)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 11-step recipe (with ACES + IoU), in Python form:

       def run_11_step_recipe(g, ref_img=None):
           """Apply the canonical 11-step recipe to a built model."""
           # Step 1-7: build the model (caller's responsibility)
           # Step 8: validate schema
           audit = lblSpec.lblValidateAgainstSpec(g)
           if audit["blocking"]:
               raise ValueError("BLOCKING audit failures")
           # Step 9: run ACES
           try:
               lblSpec.aces.run({"scene": g})
           except Exception as e:
               print(f"ACES skipped: {e}")
           # Step 10: IoU check (image-driven only)
           if ref_img is not None:
               iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
               if iou < 0.80:
                   g.scale.set(1.1, 1.1, 1.1)
                   iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
                   if iou < 0.80:
                       print(f"Warning: IoU {iou} after scale-up")
           # Step 11: final audit
           final = lblSpec.auditProductionCompleteness(g)
           return g, final

  The renderer runs steps 8-11 automatically on drop; the Python
  factory only needs to handle steps 1-7 (build).

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 326 — 3-VARIANT RANKING (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 166)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 3-variant / best-of-N ranking pattern:

       def build_3_variants(factory_func, *args):
           """Build 3 variants and return the best one (highest IoU)."""
           variants = []
           for seed in (1, 2, 3):
               g = factory_func(*args, seed=seed)
               iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
               warnings = lblSpec.lblValidateAgainstSpec(g)["warnings"]
               variants.append({"g": g, "iou": iou, "warnings": len(warnings)})
           # Sort by IoU (high) and warnings (low)
           variants.sort(key=lambda v: (-v["iou"], v["warnings"]))
           return variants[0]["g"]

  Most Python factories skip this; the renderer's auto-ranker
  runs on drop if the factory exposes a `seed` parameter.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 327 — 7 PRODUCTION TECHNIQUES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 293.8 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 31.8)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 7 production techniques, in Python form:

  1. DETAIL INVENTORY TRACKING
       g.userData.sculptRuntime = {
           "detailInventory": [
               {"id": "head", "region": "head", "kind": "feature",
                "priority": "high", "reviewThreshold": 0.7},
               ...
           ],
       }
       # Validator checks every high-priority entry has a matching
       # mesh.

  2. LAYERED ARMOR / OVERLAPPING PLATES
       # Use MT_advanced.makeInstanced for repeated plates
       plates = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(plate_geo, 12, {
           "transforms": [
               {"position": [0.2, y, 0.1], "rotation": [0, 0.1, 0],
                "scale": [1, 1, 1]}
               for y in [0.5, 0.6, 0.7, ...]
           ],
       })

  3. MULTI-LAYER CLOTH
       # Outer skin + inner detail
       outer = THREE.Mesh.new(outer_geo, cloth_mat)
       inner = THREE.Mesh.new(inner_geo, detail_mat)
       inner.scale.set(0.95, 0.95, 0.95)
       g.add(outer)
       g.add(inner)

  4. MICRO-CHIPS + DECALS
       from js import document, THREE
       canvas = document.createElement("canvas")
       # ... draw decal ...
       decal_tex = THREE.CanvasTexture.new(canvas)
       mat = THREE.MeshStandardMaterial.new({
           "color": 0xffffff, "map": decal_tex, "transparent": True,
       })

  5. SILHOUETTE PASS
       from js import lblSpec
       silhouette = lblSpec.helpers.captureSilhouette(g, "front")
       if silhouette["issues"]:
           print("Silhouette issues:", silhouette["issues"])

  6. MATERIAL VARIANTS
       # Style A: lbl.define_palette with multiple entries
       lbl.define_palette({
           "shell_a":  {...},
           "shell_b":  {...},  # variant
           "accent_a": {...},
           "accent_b": {...},  # variant
       })

  7. PRODUCTION-COMPLETENESS AUDIT
       from js import lblSpec
       report = lblSpec.auditProductionCompleteness(g)
       if report["missing_identity_parts"]:
           for part in report["missing_identity_parts"]:
               print(f"Missing: {part}")

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 328 — 5-LOD CHAIN (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 292 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 188)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5-LOD chain (LOD0-LOD4) in Python form:

       from js import MT_advanced, THREE

       def build_5_lod_chain(base_geo, mat):
           """Generate 5 LODs from a high-poly base."""
           lods = MT_advanced.generateLOD(base_geo, {
               "levels": [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05],
               "minVerts": 100,
           })
           lod_obj = THREE.LOD.new()
           for i, level_geo in enumerate(lods):
               mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(level_geo, mat)
               lod_obj.addLevel(mesh, i * 10)  # distance threshold
           return lod_obj

  Use:
       lod = build_5_lod_chain(dense_geom, mat)
       g.add(lod)
       # The renderer auto-switches LOD based on camera distance.

  When to use 5-LOD:
       - Models with > 10,000 tris
       - Real-time scenes with many models visible
       - Mobile / VR targets

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 329 — GLB-FIRST PROFESSIONAL PIPELINE (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 44)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The GLB-first professional pipeline in Python form:

       from js import lblSpec

       def export_to_glb(g, filename="model.glb"):
           """Export the model to GLB format."""
           from js import GLTFExporter
           exporter = GLTFExporter.new()
           # GLTFExporter is async; use a callback or await
           def on_done(result):
               # result is a Blob containing the GLB
               url = URL.createObjectURL(result)
               # Trigger download
               link = document.createElement("a")
               link.href = url
               link.download = filename
               link.click()
           exporter.parse(
               g, on_done,
               {"binary": True, "embedImages": True},
           )

  Python factories that produce hero-quality models should also:
       - Use BVH-accelerated raycasting (PART 188):
         from js import MeshBVH
         bvh = MeshBVH.new(geom)
       - Include look-dev lights in the model (PART 187):
         lblSpec.userData.lookDevLights = create_sport_motorcycle_look_dev_lights("reference")
       - Set the 5-field style pair (PART 189):
         bp["meta"]["style"] = "chunky-mascot"
         bp["meta"]["subject"] = "sport-motorcycle"
         bp["meta"]["sourceReference"] = "bd5da92411032501f007edb073f06372.jpg"
         bp["meta"]["referenceViews"] = "isometric-front-right"
         bp["meta"]["lblVersion"] = "v1.36 / v8.27"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 330 — REFINEMENT + STYLE LOCKING + VARIANT RANKING (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 43)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 3-iteration refinement cycle (PART 43) in Python form:

       def refine_model(g, ref_img, max_iters=3):
           """Refine the model iteratively to match the reference."""
           for i in range(max_iters):
               iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
               if iou >= 0.85:
                   return g  # Acceptable
               if iou < 0.50:
                   # Major rebuild — too far off
                   return _major_rebuild(g, ref_img)
               # Mid-range: apply targeted fix
               diff = lblSpec.helpers.getIoUDiff(g, ref_img)
               if "head" in diff["misaligned"]:
                   # Scale up head
                   head_pivot = g.userData.sculptRuntime["nodes"]["head"]
                   head_pivot.scale.set(1.05, 1.05, 1.05)
               elif "limbs" in diff["misaligned"]:
                   # Re-loft limbs with thicker profile
                   re_loft_limbs(g)
           return g

  Style locking (PART 43.2):
       # The renderer's lookDevLights() factory reads meta.style
       # and applies the matching style lock automatically. For
       # manual override:
       g.userData.meta = g.userData.meta or {}
       g.userData.meta["style"] = "chunky-mascot"
       g.userData.meta["styleLock"] = True  # disable style override

  Variant ranking (PART 43.3):
       # Same as PART 326 — generate 3 variants, pick the best.

PART 339-345 — v1.36 / v8.27 PYTHON ADAPTATION: ANYCREATURE INTEGRATION
================================================================================

PART 75-100 of Prompt_To_Ts.txt covers the anyCreature 1.3.1
integration — the canonical character/creature pipeline for the
LBL renderer. PART 339-345 here is the Python adaptation: how
Python factories can OPT INTO the anyCreature pipeline by emitting
the right userData shape.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 339 — ANYCREATURE USERDATA CONTRACT (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 90-94)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The anyCreature 1.3.1 compiler reads a specific userData shape
  on the returned Group. Python factories opt in by emitting it.

       from js import THREE

       def opt_into_anycreature(g, character_class="humanoid"):
           """Set up the userData shape anyCreature expects."""
           g.userData.anycreature = {
               "version": "1.3.1",
               "characterClass": character_class,  # "humanoid" | "quadruped" | "avian" | "custom"
               "skeleton": g.userData.sculptRuntime.get("nodes", {}),
               "skinnedMeshes": g.userData.sculptRuntime.get("meshes", {}),
               "detailInventory": g.userData.sculptRuntime.get("detailInventory", []),
           }
           return g

  anyCreature will then:
    1. Run buildSkeleton() to validate the rig.
    2. Run buildPart() / buildFin() / buildEye() for each
       detail inventory entry.
    3. Apply audit #E186 (sculptRuntime conformance).
    4. Compute LBS / DQS skin weights.
    5. Add any missing parts based on the character class.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 340 — ANYCREATURE 4 ROOT JOINTS PITFALL (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 90-100 + ANALYSIS_REPORT.txt #1)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  KNOWN ISSUE from ANALYSIS_REPORT.txt #1:
  `public/rig/skeletons/object.json` has 4 root joints (Bone_Top /
  Bottom / Left / Right) and violates the 1-root rule. The
  anycreature compiler will throw on buildSkeleton.

  Python workaround:
       from js import THREE

       def ensure_single_root(g):
           """Combine multiple root joints into a single root."""
           roots = [c for c in g.children
                    if isinstance(c, THREE.Bone)]
           if len(roots) > 1:
               # Create a single root bone
               new_root = THREE.Bone.new()
               new_root.name = "root"
               g.add(new_root)
               for r in roots:
                   g.remove(r)
                   new_root.add(r)
                   # Set position to the original root's position
                   new_root.position.set(0, 0, 0)

  Apply before opt_into_anycreature().

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 341 — 4-LEVEL RIGGING INTEGRATION (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 75-89)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 4-level rigging integration (LBS, DQS, implicit, delta mush)
  in Python form:

       from js import MT_organic, THREE

       def apply_4_level_rig(geom, skeleton, method="lbs"):
           """Apply one of 4 skinning methods."""
           if method == "lbs":
               return MT_organic.computeLBS(skeleton, geom)
           elif method == "dqs":
               return MT_organic.computeDQS(skeleton, geom)
           elif method == "implicit":
               return MT_organic.implicitSkin(geom, {"envelope": "smooth"})
           elif method == "delta_mush":
               # Apply after one of the above
               skinned = MT_organic.computeLBS(skeleton, geom)
               return MT_organic.deltaMush(skinned, {
                   "iterations": 3, "steps": 5,
               })

  Choose the method based on the model:
    - LBS: default, fast, good for most chunky-mascot characters
    - DQS: better for twisting joints (elbows, knees)
    - implicit: smoothest, best for organic skin
    - delta_mush: post-processing pass to fix LBS bulge

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 342 — SKELETON CLASS + TEMPLATE LIBRARY (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 76)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The skeleton class + template library in Python form:

       from js import THREE

       class SkeletonTemplate:
           def __init__(self, name):
               self.name = name
               self.bones = []
           def add_bone(self, name, position, parent_name=None):
               bone = THREE.Bone.new()
               bone.name = name
               bone.position.set(*position)
               if parent_name:
                   parent = self.find_bone(parent_name)
                   parent.add(bone)
               else:
                   self.bones.append(bone)
               return bone
           def find_bone(self, name):
               for b in self.bones:
                   if b.name == name:
                       return b
               return None
           def to_three_skeleton(self):
               return THREE.Skeleton.new(self.bones)

       # Usage: humanoid template
       t = SkeletonTemplate("humanoid")
       t.add_bone("pelvis", [0, 0, 0])
       t.add_bone("spine", [0, 0.3, 0], "pelvis")
       t.add_bone("chest", [0, 0.6, 0], "spine")
       t.add_bone("head", [0, 0.9, 0], "chest")
       t.add_bone("arm_L", [0.2, 0.5, 0], "chest")
       t.add_bone("arm_R", [-0.2, 0.5, 0], "chest")
       t.add_bone("leg_L", [0.1, 0, 0], "pelvis")
       t.add_bone("leg_R", [-0.1, 0, 0], "pelvis")
       skeleton = t.to_three_skeleton()

  Other templates shipped:
       - "quadruped" (4 legs, spine, head, tail)
       - "avian" (2 legs, 2 wings, beak, tail feathers)
       - "fish" (no legs, fins, tail)
       - "serpent" (no legs, long body, head, tail)
       - "humanoid" (above)
       - "custom" (user-defined)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 343 — SEMANTIC LANDMARK RATIOS (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 21)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 11 semantic landmark ratios (PART 21) in Python form:

       LANDMARK_RATIOS = {
           # Body proportions (for humanoid characters)
           "W_over_H": 0.5,           # width / total height
           "head_share_of_length": 0.25,  # head height / total
           "leg_fraction": 0.5,        # legs / total
           "torso_fraction": 0.30,     # torso / total
           "arm_fraction": 0.45,       # arms (reach) / total
           "shoulder_to_hip_ratio": 1.2,
           # Head proportions
           "head_W_over_H": 0.75,
           "eye_spacing_over_W": 0.5,
           "nose_length_over_H": 0.33,
           # Vehicle proportions
           "wheelbase_over_length": 0.6,
           "track_over_wheelbase": 0.4,
           "ground_clearance_over_W": 0.15,
       }

  Use in meta.proportions:
       bp["meta"]["proportions"] = {
           "W_over_H": 0.5,
           "head_share_of_length": 0.25,
           "leg_fraction": 0.5,
       }

  The validator checks that the values are plausible (within
  expected ranges for the archetype). For a chunky-mascot
  character, W_over_H should be 0.4-0.6, head_share_of_length
  should be 0.2-0.3, etc.

(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 22)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Subject routing decides which model class / archetype the AI
  factory should produce. The 11 canonical categories:

       CATEGORY_ROUTING = {
           "character":     "humanoid / creature / mascot",
           "vehicle":       "car / motorcycle / plane / boat / train",
           "architecture":  "building / interior / landscape",
           "weapon":        "knife / gun / sword / staff",
           "creature":      "beast / insect / fish / bird",
           "plant":         "tree / flower / grass",
           "furniture":     "chair / table / bed / shelf",
           "prop":          "container / tool / toy / food",
           "environment":   "rocks / sky / ground / water",
           "abstract":      "shape / pattern / sculpture",
           "other":         "anything else",
       }

  Apply via meta.category:
       bp["meta"]["category"] = "vehicle"
       bp["meta"]["category"] = "creature"
       # etc.

  The router uses meta.category to pick the right detail-inventory
  template (PART 331-338).

PART 345 — POST-RENDER FIDELITY SELF-SCORE (PYTHON FORM)
(extends Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 23)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 5-metric fidelity self-score, in Python form:

       from js import lblSpec

       def compute_fidelity_score(g, ref_img):
           """Return a 0..1 fidelity score for image-driven factories."""
           iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
           silhouette_match = lblSpec.helpers.checkSilhouette(g, ref_img)
           color_match = lblSpec.helpers.checkColorPalette(g, ref_img)
           proportion_match = lblSpec.helpers.checkProportions(g, ref_img)
           detail_match = lblSpec.helpers.checkDetailInventory(g)
           # Weighted average
           score = (
               0.30 * iou +
               0.25 * silhouette_match +
               0.20 * color_match +
               0.15 * proportion_match +
               0.10 * detail_match
           )
           return {
               "total": score,
               "iou": iou,
               "silhouette": silhouette_match,
               "color": color_match,
               "proportion": proportion_match,
               "detail": detail_match,
           }

  Use:
       score = compute_fidelity_score(g, ref_img)
       if score["total"] < 0.80:
           # Refine and retry
           g = refine_model(g, ref_img, max_iters=3)

PART 346-352 — v1.36 / v8.27 PYTHON ADAPTATION: STAGED BUILD PASSES
================================================================================

PART 19 of Prompt_To_Ts.txt documents the img2threejs staged build
passes — a 6-pass pipeline that builds an image-driven model in
stages. PART 346-352 here is the Python adaptation: the 6 passes
in Python form, plus the 8 audit items from PART 24-26.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 346 — 6 STAGED BUILD PASSES (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 19 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 19)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The 6 staged build passes (PASS 1-6) in Python form:

       def run_6_pass_build(ref_img):
           """Build an image-driven model in 6 staged passes."""
           g = THREE.Group.new()
           g.name = "Multi-pass Build"

           # PASS 1: silhouette (just the outline)
           silhouette = lblSpec.helpers.extractSilhouette(ref_img)
           silhouette_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(
               THREE.ShapeGeometry.new(silhouette), mat_silhouette,
           )
           g.add(silhouette_mesh)

           # PASS 2: bounding boxes (one per major volume)
           bboxes = lblSpec.helpers.detectBoundingBoxes(ref_img)
           for i, bb in enumerate(bboxes):
               box_mesh = THREE.Mesh.new(
                   THREE.BoxGeometry.new(bb.w, bb.h, bb.d), mat_bbox,
               )
               box_mesh.position.set(bb.x, bb.y, bb.z)
               box_mesh.name = f"bbox_{i}"
               g.add(box_mesh)

           # PASS 3: major volumes (replace bboxes with real geometry)
           for i, bb in enumerate(bboxes):
               # Determine the right primitive for this volume
               prim = lblSpec.helpers.suggestPrimitive(ref_img, bb)
               g.children[i + 1] = build_volume(bb, prim, mat)

           # PASS 4: detail meshes (rivets, decals)
           rivets = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(rivet_geo, 8, {...})
           g.add(rivets)

           # PASS 5: material application
           for child in g.children:
               if hasattr(child, "geometry"):
                   child.material = lblSpec.helpers.suggestMaterial(
                       ref_img, child.name,
                   )

           # PASS 6: lighting + final audit
           lights = MT_lookdev.createCharacterLookDevLights("reference")
           for light in lights["lights"]:
               g.add(light)
           audit = lblSpec.auditProductionCompleteness(g)
           return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 347 — PIXEL-PERFECT FIDELITY PASS (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 18 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 18)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The pixel-perfect fidelity pass from PART 18, in Python form:

       def pixel_perfect_pass(g, ref_img, threshold=0.05):
           """Adjust the model to match the reference image at the
           pixel level. Up to 10 iterations."""
           for i in range(10):
               iou = lblSpec.helpers.compareToReference(g, ref_img)
               if iou >= 0.95:
                   return g
               # Find the largest misaligned region
               diff = lblSpec.helpers.getIoUDiff(g, ref_img)
               region = max(diff["misaligned_regions"],
                            key=lambda r: r["area"])
               # Adjust the region
               if region["id"] in g.userData.sculptRuntime["nodes"]:
                   pivot = g.userData.sculptRuntime["nodes"][region["id"]]
                   # Apply the diff
                   pivot.position.x += region["dx"] * threshold
                   pivot.position.y += region["dy"] * threshold
                   pivot.scale.set(
                       1 + region["scale_x"] * threshold,
                       1 + region["scale_y"] * threshold,
                       1,
                   )
           return g

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 348 — LANDMARK PIXEL MEASUREMENT (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 21 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 21)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The landmark pixel measurement pattern, in Python form:

       def measure_landmarks(ref_img, landmarks):
           """Measure pixel positions of key landmarks in the reference image.
           landmarks = [{"name": "head_center", "x": 0.5, "y": 0.2}, ...]
           Returns a dict of {name: (world_x, world_y, world_z)}."""
           from js import lblSpec
           measurements = {}
           for lm in landmarks:
               # Convert image pixel to world position
               world_pos = lblSpec.helpers.imageToWorld(
                   [lm["x"], lm["y"]], ref_img,
                   [0, 0, 0],  # camera position
                   [0, 0, 1],  # camera forward
               )
               measurements[lm["name"]] = world_pos
           return measurements

  Use:
       landmarks = [
           {"name": "head_center", "x": 0.5, "y": 0.2},
           {"name": "left_arm",    "x": 0.3, "y": 0.5},
           {"name": "right_arm",   "x": 0.7, "y": 0.5},
           {"name": "left_leg",    "x": 0.4, "y": 0.8},
           {"name": "right_leg",   "x": 0.6, "y": 0.8},
       ]
       world = measure_landmarks(ref_img, landmarks)
       head_pivot.position.set(*world["head_center"])
       # ...

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 349 — RENDERER GUARANTEES NOT ON THIS PATH (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 24 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 24)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  Renderer guarantees that DON'T apply to the Python path:

    1. JS closure over the Group         — Python uses a class
    2. import.meta.url resolution         — Pyodide has its own
    3. import map for Three.js addons      — Pyodide uses window globals
    4. Async import()                     — Python uses asyncio.ensure_future
    5. Worker-based TS compile            — Python runs in main thread
    6. Dynamic import of helpers          — Python's `from js import`
    7. ESM module loading                 — Python files are .py
    8. Service worker caching             — Pyodide caches in-memory

  See PART 24 in Prompt_To_Ts.txt for the full audit.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
PART 350 — TS PIXEL-PERFECT FIDELITY (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 18 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 18)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The TS-specific pixel-perfect techniques from PART 18, in Python
  form. Most are already in PART 347-348; PART 350 here adds the
  Python-specific notes:

    - 18.1 — colorVariation/toneVariant: Python factories should
            NOT auto-apply these (they override the artist's
            intent). Set them manually if needed.
    - 18.2 — auto-side-camera: Python factories don't have a
            "side camera" concept; use the silhouette test
            (PART 248.1) instead.
    - 18.3 — mipmap generation: Three.js auto-generates mipmaps;
            Python factories don't need to call generateMipmaps.
    - 18.4 — aspect-ratio-locked cameras: Use the lblSpec
            camera API (lblSpec.camera.lockAspectRatio = True).

PART 352 — CHARACTER / MASCOT SCULPT ENRICHMENT (PYTHON FORM)
(extends PART 293 + Prompt_To_Ts.txt PART 31)
──────────────────────────────────────────────

  The character / mascot sculpt enrichment in Python form. The 5
  layers (PART 293.1-293.5) are the canonical recipe.

       def enrich_character_sculpt(g, archetype="chunky_mascot"):
           """Apply the 5-layer sculpt enrichment to a character model."""
           # Layer 1: BASE MESH (already done by the factory)
           # Layer 2: BAKED DETAIL
           from js import MT_materials
           if archetype == "chunky_mascot":
               for name, mesh in g.userData.sculptRuntime["meshes"].items():
                   if "head" in name or "body" in name:
                       normal = MT_materials.bakeMap(
                           mesh.geometry, "normal", {"size": 1024})
                       ao = MT_materials.bakeMap(
                           mesh.geometry, "ao", {"size": 1024})
                       mesh.material.normalMap = normal
                       mesh.material.aoMap = ao
                       mesh.material.aoMapIntensity = 0.5

           # Layer 3: SCULPTED DETAIL
           from js import MT_mesh
           for name, mesh in g.userData.sculptRuntime["meshes"].items():
               if "head" in name:
                   # Add detail around the face
                   mesh.geometry = MT_mesh.sculptBrush(
                       mesh.geometry, {
                           "centre": [0, 0, 0.2], "radius": 0.15,
                           "mode": "smooth", "strength": 0.2,
                       },
                   )

           # Layer 4: ACCESSORY LAYER
           rivet_geo = THREE.SphereGeometry.new(0.013, 6, 4)
           from js import MT_advanced
           rivets = MT_advanced.makeInstanced(rivet_geo, 8, {
               "transforms": [
                   {"position": [math.cos(a) * 0.3, 0.0, math.sin(a) * 0.3],
                    "rotation": [0, 0, 0], "scale": [1, 1, 1]}
                   for a in [i * 2 * math.pi / 8 for i in range(8)]
               ],
           })
           g.add(rivets)

           # Layer 5: LIGHTING (renderer auto-applies based on meta.style)
           return g
